# LEDGER.md — the novel-results ledger of the Hadwiger–Nelson workspace

*Extracted from `CLAUDE.md` § Frontier on 2026-09-22 (Kooshi), so that what is looked up by address lives in a file and what is
reasoned from stays in context. **This file is not imported into `CLAUDE.md`; it is read at two moments** — the re-grokking
(WORKFLOW 2.0 step 3.1, every row's mechanism walked from inside the milestone's question, `CLAUDE.md` and this file open side by
side) and the close (step 9, the new row written here, "where the problem stands" rewritten there) — and consulted by row number
whenever a brief names a kernel consumer or a control value (a brief cites the row; the implementer reads the row, or better the
Lean module it names). Grades are `CLAUDE.md` § Notation's; the one-home-per-fact rule puts numbers, certificates, names and
addresses HERE and mechanism and readings in the grokkings, each pointing at the other; `tools/claude_md_fact_audit.py` audits
`CLAUDE.md` and this file as one corpus. Current state only, like `CLAUDE.md`: a corrected row is rewritten in place and the story
goes to git.*

*The 2026-09-25 crunch's three milestones are folded in: rows 98–102 (M1, FS-91), 103–107 (M2, the kernel lane) and 108–110
(M3), then row 88's question (rows 111–113) and FS-67 (rows 114–115), closed later the same day; its index is `plans/CRUNCH-2026-09-25-INDEX.md` and its items `plans/FS91-close-inventory.md`, `plans/M2-close-inventory.md` and
`plans/M3-close-inventory.md`.*

## The claim list — what is ours beyond Theorems A and B (moved from `CLAUDE.md` § The prior-art position, 2026-09-26)

*`CLAUDE.md` keeps the position's lineage sentence, Theorems A and B with `[Fischer1994]`'s value, every kernel certificate, and a pointer here;
this is the rest of its **Ours** list, verbatim, beside the rows it names, and its whole **Not ours** list, verbatim (moved 2026-09-26, E1,
design (b)), which `CLAUDE.md` says to read before calling anything ours: it guards against misclaims.*

**Ours:** the sieve; the lift theory; the compulsion transfer and distance law; the tightness mechanism;
the fan-gap transport and its compulsion consumers (row 66); the tight/slack classifier of a residue plane's
compulsions and its propagation up a tower (row 67, G54); plane freedom at every prime p ≡ 3 (mod 4); the field-internal reading of
the fractional blow-up and `χ_f(K²) = 4` (row 71); the 3-adic barrier and the four-colour lower bounds for real quadratic
fields (rows 72–73); the fibre-share inequality and the all-levels certificates, with the answer to the `p`-adic Hadwiger–Nelson question (row 75); the forced group of de Grey's lattice (the lattice form of Hubai's 2018 ring computation) and its census; the three-colour criterion, density and dichotomy for abelian Cayley graphs and the `Θ` certificate (rows 78–79); the classification of four-colour edge-local heights over every generating set, the four no-gos by format and the rank-1 one-way walls and criterion, and `L21cc6506`'s looseness at four colours (rows 80–81); the passage from additive to all three-colourings at the field level and the census's structural certificates (rows 82–83; not found in print — `[Johnson2000]` p. 176 poses the family as open and `[Johnson2006]` p. 214 asks whether any `m` needs four, both read 2026-09-27); `F = P = 8Λ` on de Grey's lattice by derivation and the audit of its input, the triangle-free abstract rigidity and its Soifer calibration, and the no-gos on `Λ` (rows 85–87); the type-(B) frame, the classification of `K²`'s four-colourings, the rigid local plane and the no-go family on type-(B) planes (rows 88–94); rational band rigidity at two or more primes, at every band, and its field consequences (rows 95–97, 98); the class theorem for character colourings, the character chromatic numbers, three colours over every real number field and one prime's three failures (rows 99–102); rank one at four colours reduced to single colourings, the mixing selection and the windows at five over ℚ(√167) (rows 108–110); the product criterion `χ(E_k(G)) ≤ k` for gap-free `G`, its `K_k × X` form and the sharp mixing pullback for every gap-free `G` (rows 114–115; as far as the gate reached, Duffus–Sands–Woodrow 1985 unread); prime inversion in valuation form, with no number field (row 105); four-colour rigidity split into invariant measures and transience, Theorem C over the real S2 fields and its ℚ(√47) form (rows 111–113); Kronecker selection refuted as stated, its reduction and its corrected form's reduction to one density step (row 116); T-C(A)'s class among real quadratic fields and SP-Cor over it (row 117); rank one of the selection and the non-irreducibility of the unit-vector group on its solenoid (row 118); the winding form of the three-colour criterion's ⟹ as a `Θ₃` certificate's semantics, and the certificate's kernel checker (row 119; as far as the gate reached; its ingredients are in print); the entropy split of measure rigidity for the unit-vector group and its Einsiedler–Fish transfer, and `χ(ℚ(√47)²) = 5` (rows 120–122); the class theorem and SP-Cor in every degree, so the local–global conjecture's main clause through five over every real number field, `χ(ℚ(√3,√5)²) = 5`, the type-(B) fields' rotation-invariant half and the reduction of six at ℚ(√167) to `χ_CP(ℝ²) ≥ 6` (rows 123–125) (`χ_CP(ℝ²) ≥ 6` itself, and `χ(ℝ²) = χ_m⁰(ℝ²)` with no hypothesis, are `[OAIMath2026]`'s) — not found in print (X24R; `[Johnson2000]` and `[Johnson2006]` read 2026-09-27, not found in either); TR-ROT's equivalence with Q-μ and its spectral closure on `E` (row 126; prior art not searched); the kernel form of the main clause through five over named hypotheses, with `χ(ℚ(√47)²) ≤ 5` unconditional (row 127; the upper half is row 52's colouring).

**Not ours:** band rigidity on `ℤ[i][1/P₅, 1/P₁₃]` (`[Manners2015]` Lemma 5.3), the regular chromatic number (`[BarajasSerra2008]`); the mixing selection's complete-graph case (Burr–Erdős–Lovász, `[Tardif2008]` Thm 3.2) and its threshold beside `K_k` (`[PakVilenchik2013]`); Duffus–Sands–Woodrow's `B(n) ⟹ H(n)`, so with `[Tardif2023]` `B(n)` and `A(n)` fail for `n ≥ 4` (a composition of print), and the vector pullbacks under rows 114–115 (`[GodsilEtAl2020]` Thm 4.16); the ratio-only counterexample (`[Zhu2021]` Thm 3); one-dimensional SFT facts (Lind–Marcus); closed-set rigidity on the circle and its disjointness input (`[Furstenberg1967]`); for hyperbolic multi-parameter actions, row 95's method and the isometric direction's minimal circles (`[Berend1983]`, `[Berend1984]`, `[Berend1984min]`); the zero-entropy pigeonhole for multiplier semigroups of polynomial growth (`[EinsiedlerFish2010]`, after Sataev 1975) and the positive-entropy dichotomy for totally irreducible solenoid actions (`[EinsiedlerLindenstrauss2022]` Thm 1.2, EL2003); the four-colour grid's cohomological triviality (`[CGHO2025coh]`), the triangular lattice's four-colour height (`[MooreNewman2000]`), the Zig-zag Theorem (`[SimonyiTardos2006]`); the periodic-colouring question (`[Ellenberg2018]`; Gibbs and Mixon on Polymath16, 2018); the height lift behind the three-colour theorem (Chandgotia's height functions for four-cycle-free targets); the forced pair at 8/3 (Harder, Polymath16, 2018); the Moser ring's 9,120 four-colourings, all 8-periodic (Hubai, Polymath16, 2018); the no-monochromatic-√3-triple trees and the stripe lemma (`[Parts2020hv]`, §3); the annihilator method row 85's periodicity step instantiates, and its one-family exception (`[KariSzabados2016]`, `[HervaKari2023]`, after Puzynina); MIS-normality of categorical products (`[Zhang2012]` Thm 1.8) and optimal vector colourings in the least eigenspace (`[GodsilRobersonRooney2017]`, FS38-D2-A(ii) in vector words); the clopen correspondence (`[BergelsonMoragues2021]`), the measure centre (`[ChenDai2017]`), frozen colourings (`[AlonEtAl2021]`), the measurable Hoffman bound (`[BachocEtAl2014]`), the split-place hyperbola computation (`[BardestaniMallahiKarai2017]` §3.2) and the finite norm property of subrings of number fields (`[LevitzMott1972]`); stripes from no monochromatic √3-triangle and the abandoned route to period 16 (Gibbs, Polymath16, 2018); the renormalisation (Tao, the wiki's Cor. 9); `K_k × Y` uniquely colourable (`[KlotzSander2017]` Thm 4.2, after Greenwell–Lovász); the forced pair at 8/9 and its spindle into K(√7) (Parts, Polymath16, 2021); the value χ(K²) = 4 and the additive ℤ/4-colouring of the plane over his congruence family of biquadratic fields (`[Fischer1994]`, § Publication; restated in `[Johnson2000]` p. 176 and `[Johnson2006]` p. 214); Theorem A's coset construction out of the unit-vector subgroup (`[Fischer1990]` Thm 1(i); for every `G(S, D)`, `[Johnson2006]` p. 213; first for `ℚ²` and `ℚ³`, `[BendaPerles2000]` pp. 120–121; metric-free, over any abelian group and any set of differences, at target `ℤ/n` with value 1 on the differences, `[Johnson1982]`'s Theorem (1982)); the unit-vector ring of ℚ, `ℤ[i][1/p : p ≡ 1 (mod 4)]` (`[BendaPerles2000]` p. 120); the geometric fractional chromatic number, the blow-up and `G27` (`[AmbrusEtAl2023]`,
`[MatolcsiEtAl2025]`); the finite-plane bound
and χ(𝔽₁₁²) = 5 (Moorhouse, Manturov, Madore); the unit argument and the 1/k obstruction, with the type-M relation at N ≡ −1 (mod 24) that row 82 uses (Fischer, 1990, additive colourings only); the unit-vector ring's prime inversion for multiquadratic fields at odd unramified primes, the p-integrality of unit vectors behind it, the ring in closed form for real quadratic fields, and 1/2 ∈ Σ₀ iff N ≡ 7 (mod 8) (`[Fischer1990b]`, 1989, read 2026-09-16); ω and
ω ≠ χ on finite planes (Vinh, Krebs, Schneider–Silva); the cell family's ≥ 7 floor (Sokolov–Voronov); the
observed 2-adic compulsion pattern and its coordinate form (Heule, Parts, Dúcz); the two-colour mechanism over fields (Woodall, Fischer, Moorhouse, Madore; metric-free, with its converse and the parity form of every two-colouring, `[Johnson1982]`); the multiquadratic decomposition (ChatGPT, credited in row 63); the ratio bound with its equality case and the Hoffman-colouring facts (Hoffman, Delsarte, Lovász, Blokhuis–Brouwer–Haemers); the non-frozen vertex (reconfiguration folklore); the tower chain (`[Madore2015]` Prop. 3.8) and the `p`-adic question (his ¶1.5; `[BardestaniMallahiKarai2017]`); the chain theorem, prime inversion in its general form (every number field, every prime) and the two- and three-colour classifications (GPT Astra, G55); transcendence is free (`[Fischer1990]` Thm 6) and its lemma, integrality at a place whose residue field has no `√−1` (`[Madore2015]` Prop. 3.2); the bridge `χ(ℝ²) = sup_F χ(F²)` (`[BendaPerles2000]` pp. 117–118, 1976, by Tarski's completeness of real closed fields and compactness; as an embedding theorem `[Johnson2000]` §3; credited to them by `[Fischer1990]` p. 182; re-derived as `[Madore2015]` ¶5.4, whose footnote 5 credits them); the extremal-trace name for MR's finitely generated case, the generalized Furstenberg conjecture (`[LacaWarren2018]`, open); Croft's `m₁(ℝ²) ≥ 0.2293` (`[Croft1967]`); the plane's number as a measurable one with no hypothesis, six for measurable colourings proper almost everywhere, and rigidity of rotation-invariant laws on the dual of the algebraic plane (`[OAIMath2026]` family 158, 2026).

## A. The novel-results ledger

### The novel-results ledger

*Kernel-checked unless marked (grades: § Notation). Row numbers are stable identifiers cited across `plans/`: never renumber, never reuse. Lean modules
are under `Proofs/HadwigerNelson/`. Paths under `build/` are local, untracked, regenerable measurement
artifacts — provenance, never a certificate; the certificate is the Lean module.*

| # | Result | Where | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Two-circle theorem**: every unit-distance graph on the circles of radius 1 and 1/√3 about one point (the full uncountable set) is 3-colourable. | `TwoCircle.lean` (`colorable_three_induce`) | M19 |
| 2 | **Seam identity**: the cross-chord rotation between those circles is σ = (√3 + i√33)/6, of infinite order; two-circle labels form a strip ℤ/6 × ℤ. | `TwoCircle.seam_pow_ne_one` | M19 |
| 3 | **Coverage ratchet**: `CoverAtLeast` is monotone under adding edges, widening the class and lowering the level; at level k it is the fan. | `CoverageMono.lean` | M19 |
| 4 | **Fan ⟺ compulsory coverage**: a pivot is fanned iff every proper k-colouring puts all k colours on N(p) ∪ Q. | `Coverage.lean` (`fan_iff_coverage`) | M18 |
| 5 | **No class confines alone**: in-class degree ≤ 2, so class-only graphs are 3-colourable and a fourth colour frees the pivot. | `ClassGeometry.lean` (`not_fan_classOnlyGraph`) | M18 |
| 6 | **120° triangle identity**: a unit triangle lies on a circle about p iff r² = 1/3, and then p is its centroid. | `ClassGeometry.lean` (`exists_unit_triangle_iff`) | M18 |
| 7 | **Greedy colouring** (degree < k everywhere ⟹ k-colourable), absent from Mathlib. | `Greedy.lean` | M18 |
| 8 | **De Grey's graph has a balanced 5-colouring**, class sizes [317, 316, 316, 316, 316]. | `Instances/DeGreyBalanced.lean` | M19 |
| 9 | **The 120° compulsion**: in every proper 3-colouring of the two circles, three pairwise non-adjacent outer points at 120° are never monochromatic. | `Compulsion.lean` (`no_mono_outer_triple`) | M20 |
| 10 | **One-pivot cap**: {p} ∪ both circles has chromatic number exactly 4. | `Compulsion.lean` (`chromaticNumber_concentric`) | M20 |
| 11 | **Strip automaton**: two parity tubes; a recurrent core of 864 states; the colouring space grows ×12 per column; every proper colouring of a ≥ 2-column window extends to the whole tube. | `Strip.lean` (`window_extends`) | M20 |
| 12 | [tools] **Extension profile**: per pivot, every locally legal 3-pattern of the cover set tested for ambient realizability. | `tools/extend.py` | M20 |

| 18 | **Certified near-relation**: ζ₆σ⁹ misses 1 by a chord below 1/50 and is never 1. | `Glue.lean` | M22 |

its metric-free setting — any abelian group, any set of differences, the colouring of the generated subgroup copied to its cosets — is `[Johnson1982]`'s Theorem (p. 220; received 1981; target `ℤ/n`, the coefficient-sum homomorphism equal to 1 on the differences: Theorem A's case `|G| = 2` in full, with a converse, and for `n ≥ 3` only maps onto the `n`-cycle, so no colouring of a graph of chromatic number ≥ 4); the metric-free statement with an arbitrary target is the one-line merge of `[Johnson1982]` with `[Fischer1990]` Thm 1(i), not found as one statement as far as read; ours is the kernel proof (`CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §1.2; read 2026-09-27, a second reading the same day). **B's VALUE is `[Fischer1994]`'s** (1994; read in full and verified 2026-09-22 — every lemma by hand, the theorem on 181,160 unit vectors by `tools/fischer1994_check.py`): an additive ℤ/4-colouring of the whole plane over his congruence family of biquadratic fields (`CLAUDE.md` § Publication states it), the Moser spindle below — **a different colouring from B's 𝔽₄-valued residue map** (exponent 4 against 2; it separates the distance-2 pair (0, 2P) at his Moser vertex P, which every residue colouring identifies; G41), and his family a proper subfamily of what A four-colours. So "B answers" below means "B's kernel proof re-establishes what `[Fischer1994]` answered in 1994 and none of the posers knew". **B answers** `[Moorhouse2010]` §1, `[CranstonRabern2015]` (and with `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]` their first question, χ_f(K²) = 4, row 71) and `[ExooIsmailescu2019]`'s closing question, and settles one field of Benda–Perles Open Problem 11.6 (`[Soifer2024]` ch. 11), and closes `[Madore2015]` Prop. 4.6's 4 ≤ χ ≤ 5. **A's reach is not limited to the plane, and prior art's "dimension > 2 gives nothing" is about finite FIELDS only** [tools, exact; the mathematics is prior art]: `colorable_of_residue` asks for an abelian group, a subgroup and an additive map — no ring, no metric, **no commutativity** — so it takes a quaternionic input unchanged. With `M = ℚ⁴` the rational quaternions, `A` the Hurwitz order localized at 2 (odd denominators inverted), and `ψ` the reduction at the unique two-sided prime `𝔓 = (1+i)H` above 2, where `H/𝔓 ≅ 𝔽₄`, every rational quaternion of norm 1 lies in `A` with nonzero residue, giving **`χ(ℚ⁴) ≤ 4`** — Benda–Perles's value (`[BendaPerles2000]`), whose matching lower bound is the rational unit tetrahedron `0, 1, (1+i+j+k)/2, (1+i+j−k)/2`. `[Madore2015]` Remark 3.5 excludes `d > 2` because a quadratic form in ≥ 3 variables over a finite **field** is always isotropic; dimension four escapes that because the anisotropy sits in a **noncommutative** division algebra at 2 — the same algebra, `(−1,−1)`, whose ramification at a place above 2 keeps the 2-adic wall standing there without being the wall (G35) — and not in a residue field. **Not formalized and not new** (§3's `normOne` lemmas are stated over commutative rings and would need the noncommutative form; the colouring theorem itself needs no change): a scope fact about what A already covers, for FS-20. Also: (i) the endpoint bound `1/\|sin θ\|` governs cross-edge endpoints, not a base's extent; (ii) growing a base is monotone — colourings restrict, so the interface pattern set only shrinks (`10²¹ → 10⁴` for a 2.6× base); (iii) the circle sits at `√(1−1/(4n))` of the bound, on the arena boundary in every member, so the free-circle theorem's hypothesis has never been met by any object built; (iv) a two-case classification of monochromatic cross-edge kinds over `V = n(g−h)² + gh` (case A for every `n`; case B iff `v_𝔭(gh) < v_𝔭(n)`), 12/12 families retrodicted; (v) each conjugate condition beyond the first costs 2.0–2.6× in realised connectivity; (vi) a one-sided kill test for phase H2's interface reduction, milliseconds from one base colouring. **The premise is confirmed where it matters**: at the record's own `n = 4`, dropping the shape takes monochromatic off-diagonal cross edges **0 → 144** (195,961 points, 492/492 confirmed) — the shape, not the rotation, was the obstruction. **And the direction closes**: on Parts's shaped lattice every member with a nonempty circle is odd (dead by Theorem B) or `4 \| n` (immune), so the shape-free construction reaches only members this route kills; `n = 16` is the cheapest 6-open immune member and is realisable shaped at 25,567 points — phase H2's own object. | 79 | **Three colours are linear: every Cayley graph of a finitely generated torsion-free abelian group is 3-colourable iff a character puts every generator in `[1/3, 2/3]`, its periodic 3-colourings are dense, and forced pairs are exactly what periodic colourings see** (FS-73's competition; the theorems `plans/FS73-derivation.md` T21–T30, T39–T45; the understanding is G71). **[hand; two independent derivations — de Bruijn and Coxeter — read by Poros; the owner checked the two deciding steps]** For `Λ` finitely generated torsion-free and `S = −S` finite generating: **(criterion)** `Cay(Λ, S)` is 3-colourable ⟺ `Θ₃ = {θ ∈ Hom(Λ, ℝ/ℤ) : θ(S) ⊆ [1/3, 2/3]} ≠ ∅` ⟺ it has a periodic (circular) 3-colouring — every 3-colouring has an integer height through `K₃`'s universal cover whose winding is realised by a point of `Θ₃`; **(density)** periodic 3-colourings are dense, so **`P = F` (C3-inherit) and C3-aper hold at three colours for the whole class, with no arithmetic of the lattice**; **(dichotomy)** `F` has finite index ⟺ the colouring space is finite ⟺ every cell of `Θ₃` is a point, and if every point has order 3 every 3-colouring is affine. 52 of FS-61's 57 lattices carry certified gapped periodic three-colourings and 3 are 3-integral, so **FS-61's null at 167 is explained** — its one ask that could refuse (the one lattice reaching degree 14 under a 2M-point cap, 1,474,541 points, three-coloured in 3.66 s) is a noisy circular colouring (T20), and its reading that 167's 3-non-integral lattices hold too few unit vectors (20–36 against 11's 108) held for the lattices it asked, while its two richest (44 and 60 unit vectors) are the two the screens left open; the owner verified), past every period the searches reached; **[cert + hand] and `L21cc6506` (60 unit vectors, denominator 420) has `Θ₃ = ∅`, so it is not 3-colourable and `χ(ℚ(√167)²) ≥ 4`** — the first bound above three where the conjecture predicts six (FS-59's first target; at four colours `L21cc6506` is four-colourable and loose, row 81) — by two independent exact certificates: implementer E's two routes (a θ-space tree of 605,203 nodes, 7.7 MB gzipped, accepted by Coxeter's unmodified checker and by E's own; and a structural certificate over the product `U = {1, v, v̄, z, z̄}·U_R`, 4,768 Farkas-refuted cases, 106 KB, with its own standalone checker) and verifier V2's relation-branching tree (written before reading E: 30,344 leaves over 39 relations, 149 KB gzipped, `data/fs73/verify-v2/L21cc6506-empty.json.gz`, a second structurally different tree also passing), each checked by a Fraction/int-only checker that re-enumerates the lattice's unit vectors (`tools/fs73v2_check.py`: the owner re-ran it, PASS, and five planted faults were each refused for its own reason; | 80 | **Four colours, cell by cell: no certificate of non-four-colourability comes from the colour complex one cell at a time — edge-local heights exist only on four-colourable presentations of rank ≤ 2, the loop winding is the graph's parity, parity-free face fluxes are trivial, coindex topological bounds stop at four on unit-distance graphs — and plain density of periodic four-colourings fails in rank 1** (FS-79's competition and plan; the theorems `plans/FS79-derivation.md` T3–T7, T17–T37; the understanding is G73). **The class** 𝒞: Cayley graphs of finitely generated torsion-free abelian groups, `S = −S` finite, taken in the canonical form `Λ = ⟨S⟩` (T6). **[hand; two independent derivations, de Bruijn's and Coxeter's, each key step read by Poros; exact rank checks with plants; the owner checked three steps] Four no-gos, each for its format:** (1) **edge-local heights**, in the format the advisor pinned (closed on commutator squares; winding and slope as the certificate's data; no window larger than one square tested): a non-trivial four-colour edge-local height exists **iff `S` has a sum-closed half** — `±{a}`, `±{a, 2a}` or `±{a, b, a+b}`, rank ≤ 2 — where it is Moore–Newman's height generalised, and every such graph is four-colourable, so **the format certifies non-four-colourability on no graph of 𝒞** (de Bruijn's C1 with Lemmas D and S, T18–T21; Coxeter's square lemma reached the triangle-free case independently, T24); among unit-distance lattices of rank ≥ 2 only ℤ[ζ₆] carries one; at `k ≥ 5` colours no presentation with two or more generator classes carries one, because the Kneser graph `KG(k, 2)` is connected, and at `k = 4` the extra space is exactly two-dimensional — `K₄`'s three perfect matchings, `V₄` (T22); on the colouring shift's own language the same holds under two occurrence conditions — exactly on all 9,120 of de Grey's four-colourings and on `L21cc6506`'s exhibited ones (T23, T25), so **de Grey's rigidity (`F = 8Λ`, row 78) is not an edge-local height**; (2) **the loop winding** FS73-T29 named is each relation's length parity, the same for every colouring (`Q₃ = K₄ × K₂`, T17; Coxeter, and Poros independently); (3) **face-local 2-forms, one dimension up**: 24 of `Q₃`'s 2,652 four-colourings are monopoles and kill the skyrmion class locally (T27); in the parity-free format, the only one a non-bipartite graph admits, every closed form is `dη + κ` wherever at least three directions span induced squares and 3-cubes — every triangle-free unit-distance lattice, by the rhombus lemma (T28–T29, with Poros's `n ≥ 3` repair) — and the signed flux is slaved to the charges on every non-bipartite graph (T30); (4) **the coindex topological bounds** `coind(B₀) + 1`, `coind(H) + 2`, `conn(N) + 3` are at most 4 on every K₂,₃-free graph, so on every unit-distance graph, by `[SimonyiTardos2006]`'s Zig-zag Theorem (T26). **[tools, three independent checks + hand] Rank 1:** plain density of periodic four-colourings is **false** — FS73-T30's four walls are one-way (T3) — while existence and `P = F` hold there; the invariant-measure LP on windows of length `max D + 1` is exact, so window frequencies are not controlled by `χ_f` (T4); `G(ℤ,{1,3,7,12,17,22})` refuses four colours on 33 points with `χ_f ≤ 29/8`, and a Cartesian product with ℤ makes a rank-2 one, so (Q-obs) asks for refutation rank `ρ₄ ≥ 2` (T5, T7); `P ≠ F` iff every cyclic phase is rigid and a wall exists (T35); a counterexample periodic in `r − 1` directions has two rigid ends, one chiral, never additive (T34); no counterexample among the 2,706 sets to `max D = 14`, `\|D\| ≤ 5` (T36). **De Grey's lattice:** each of its 48 non-Eisenstein unit vectors lies alone in an Eisenstein coset, so the stripes and `F = 8Λ` are a many-layer effect (T37). **Predictions** (`plans/FS79-invariant-question.md` §P, scored in §R-close.5): P-B′ (a no-go of a named kind, 0.18) won; P-A, P-A′, P-B, P-O not landed (P-C, 0.42, the partition's default, took the rest); P-G72 won; P-G71 (a K₄-free four-colour rigidity theorem with no arithmetic, 0.15) not met; P-G15 not reachable (no obstruction landed); P-MO and P-rank unscored ((Q-comp) not settled); P-persona won weakly. **Prior art:** the grid case is `[CGHO2025coh]` Thm 1.1 (cohomologically trivial iff the square group is ℤ/2), of which (1)–(2) are the edge-local shadow for every generating set; the triangular lattice's height is `[MooreNewman2000]` §III; the Zig-zag Theorem is Simonyi–Tardos's (its unit-distance corollary one line, not found stated); existence of periodic colourings in rank 1 is `[EggletonErdosSkilton1990]`. **Ours:** the classification of edge-local four-colour heights over every generating set and its Kneser mechanism at `k ≥ 5`; the parity reading of the winding; the face-local no-go and its gluing; the one-way walls and the exact rank-1 criterion; the chirality reduction as a theorem. **Not claimed:** cocycles reading windows of radius ≥ 2 (`[CGHO2025coh]` Thm 5.8's gluing route is open for 𝒞), the **index** topological bounds `ind(B₀) + 1` and `ind(H) + 2`, non-local 2-forms, window-frequency LPs in rank ≥ 2, algebraic or counting certificates; | 99 | **Three colours obey the local–global conjecture over every real number field: `χ(F²) ≤ 3 ⟺ L(F) ≤ 3`, and `χ(F²) = L(F)` whenever `L(F) ≤ 4`; a non-bipartite real number field's plane is three-chromatic iff some prime of `F` above 3 has residue field `𝔽₃`; and every three-colour band character of such a field is a residue character at one such prime ("no mixing"); `χ(ℚ(α,√7)²) = 4`, `α³ = α + 1`** (FS-91; FS91-J02-L1 to J02-C2, J02-A2.1′, J09-J1, J09-J-K, J09-J4, J09-J4-A, J09-J4-T6, J09v-8). **[hand (+ Weil, tools exact); S1 three derivations (J02, J09, J09v), S2 and the torsion theorem two, S3 three, no mixing two independent (J09; J09v blind, a different route)]** The chain: FS81-T2 lifts a three-colouring to `θ ∈ Θ₃(R_F)` (every route passes through it, and through FS73-T22's ⟹, hand); torsion extraction (FS81-T3, T4) gives `τ` with `6τ = 0`; `R/6R` is a finite ring whose image of the unit vectors contains the product of the stable residue circles (a Teichmüller section and a joint Hilbert-90 lift, extending GPT Astra's Lemma 1.1); an additive map `𝔽_{q²} → (1/3)ℤ/ℤ` non-zero on `μ_{q+1}` exists only at `q = 3` (Weil above 243, exact below), and none to `(1/2)ℤ/ℤ` at `2^f`. No Galois condition, no √3, no condition at 2. **Consequences:** a field whose primes above 3 all have residue degree ≥ 3 is never three-chromatic (FS81-T7's residual case; FS-82's field ℚ(α,√7) first, whose dyadic prime splits in `F(i)`, so it has no dyadic target at all); additive three-colourings exist iff a stable `𝔽₃` prime does (generalising `[Fischer1990]` Thm 9), additive two-colourings iff a stable dyadic prime ramified in `F(i)/F`; FS81-T6's open item settled (Fischer's colourings, up to symmetry); no point of `Θ₃(R_F)` has mixed parity and order-3 types, at any real number field. | 100 | **The class theorem for character colourings (CLG), at existence scope: for every real number field `F` and `k ≥ 2`, given band rigidity (row 98), the plane has a band `k`-character — equivalently a character `k`-colouring `x ↦ ⌊kθ(x)⌋` — iff some stable place of `F(i)` carries one at some level of its residue tower; so `χ_char(F²) = min` over the residue targets, and at `k ≤ 5`, and at every `k` over fields of degree ≤ 2, a character `k`-colouring exists only if `L(F) ≤ k`** (FS-91; FS91-J07-1 to J07-15, J10-L1, J10-L2, J10-L7, J10-L8, J11-T12). **[hand (+ tools exact); two independent derivations (J07; J10 blind in phase 1, then from J07's 25 lemma statements: 24 TRUE, one GAP)]** The peel: by row 98, `θ = τ + α`; the archimedean part vanishes at every complex place and is an interval about 0 at each real one, so `τ` is band; `τ` factors through finitely many stable places (Lemma C: `R ⊗ ℤ_p = R̄_p × V_n`; Lemma C′: `R_F = O′`, the ring integral at the stable primes, with **no Galois hypothesis**, except possibly at a ramified stable dyadic place — three derivations; the exception occurs at ℚ(√5), ℚ(√17), ℚ(α)); weak approximation makes the image of `U` a product of local images; and a sum of symmetric subsets of the circle inside the band keeps **some** summand inside it (the peeling lemma, three derivations). CLG-g makes the exception explicit — a stable place carrying a band `k`-character only above level one, odd with residue field `𝔽_{p^f}`, `f ≥ 3`, `2p < k < χ(Γ_{p^f})`, or inert dyadic of norm `Q ≥ 64` — using level collapse for `k ≤ 2p` (four derivations) and every inert tower's `λ_min` scaling by `Q^{j−1}`; its corollary (g3) at `k = 7` holds only as "exact level `2e(𝔭\|3) + 1`" (a GAP in the lemmas, found by the verifier). **CLG is not a classification:** it does not say that every character colouring is pulled back from one place; that is proved at `k = 3` (row 99's no mixing) and at `k = 4` over ℚ(√59), ℚ(√83), ℚ(√131) (HD1, from CLG's *proof* with Lemma D — with no FS-66 certificate, no `[Zhang2012]`, no Lemma R), and open elsewhere (FS-92). | 147 | **In non-Siamese simple valid six-coloured fragments, vertices of at least four colours propagate along the unit circle: at a vertex `p` of four colours every semi-hub of its ring — a change point with a germ on a (Q) arc — places a vertex of at least four colours at itself or 60° from it along the ring, but for a full parallel semi-hub of three colours; paired (Q) germs of one colour pair swap inner and outer colour; at a hub the star of `p` fixes the hub's colours and missing pair, and antipodal hubs exchange tip and outer colour. | 149 | **In non-Siamese valid six-coloured fragments, simple or not, a vertex of at least four colours whose unit circle carries no (Q) arc — no arc of the boundary whose two side colours the vertex carries — exists on no disc of radius above 1 about it:** "no non-Siamese valid six-coloured fragment on a closed disc of radius above 1 about a vertex p of at least four colours has a ring carrying no (Q) arc" (FS129-SR3-QFREE-NS). | 152 | **Falconer's fan, at every number of colours, and where a five-colour fan can live; route (b)'s literal reading is empty.** "if a finite unit-distance graph H ⊂ ℝ² has a Falconer fan at k colours with apex a and tip t, |t − a| = r > 1/2, and arcsin(1/(2r))/π irrational, then every cover of ℝ² by k measurable sets has two points at distance 1 inside one of the sets, so χ_m(ℝ²) ≥ k + 1 for Falconer's everywhere-proper measurable chromatic number" (FS129-SA1-FALC-RED; `[Falconer1981]` Lemmas 1–4 checked against the paper). "let F ⊆ ℝ be a number field, E = F(t₁, …, t_n) ⊂ ℝ with t₁, …, t_n algebraically independent over F (n ≥ 0), and H ⊂ E² a finite connected unit-distance graph with apex a and tip t forming a Falconer fan at five colours; then (i) χ(F²) ≥ 4, and F has no usable prime of residue field 𝔽₇; (ii) for every usable prime 𝔭 of F of residue field 𝔽₁₁ or 𝔽₁₉, with V and φ as in FS129-SA1-PLACE-HOM at 𝔭, t − a has coordinates in V and φ(t − a) = 0" (FS129-SA1-FALC-RES; with FS129-SA1-FALC-FAN and -FALC-DICHO-K: a fan at `k` colours has `χ(H) ≥ k − 1`, and at `χ(H) = k − 1` gives apex and tip one colour). | 155 | **The (Q)-free exclusion with no map and no Siamese clause: for every measurable six-colouring proper at almost every unit pair on an open set containing the closed unit disc about a point, if four labels meet at the point in a witnessed cycle, the palettes on its unit circle cannot almost everywhere hold one of the two other labels.** The orbit form, over four points: "let U be an open set of the plane containing the closed unit disc about a point p, let c be a weak measurable six-colouring of U (a measurable map from U to six labels with c(z) ≠ c(z + u) for almost every pair (z, u) of a point z of U and a unit vector u with z + u in U), let a_0, a_1, a_2, a_3 be four of the labels and X, Y the other two, and for each j modulo 4 let q_j be a point other than p with H_{a_j} ∩ Δ({p, q_j}) = ∅ and H_{a_{j+1}} ∩ Δ({p, q_j}) = ∅, where H_a is the closed essential support of the class of a and Δ(K) is the set of z with min over q ∈ K of |z − q| below 1 and max over q ∈ K of |z − q| above 1; then there are no angle ψ and number δ > 0 such that (i) for every integer k and almost every θ with |θ − ψ − kπ/3| < δ the palette P_p(e(θ)) (`[OAIMath2026]` Definition 5.1, read at the centre p with samplings inside U) contains X or Y, and (ii) for every η > 0 the set of θ in (ψ − η, ψ + η) whose palette contains X and the set of those whose palette contains Y both have positive measure" (C132-WIT-ORBIT; C132-M-ORBIT is the same theorem over four compact sets through `p`). The ring form: "let U, p, c, the labels a_0, a_1, a_2, a_3, X, Y and the points q_0, q_1, q_2, q_3 be as in C132-WIT-ORBIT; then it is not the case that for almost every unit vector e the palette P_p(e) contains X or Y" (C132-WIT-EXCL, which is C132-M-EXCL); at an extracted centre with a cycle of four labels, C132-M-EMPTY. **[kernel] for a Borel colouring of the whole plane (FS132-L2, 2026-10-07; every step M1–M9): `HadwigerNelson.SixRing.no_outside_orbit_points` (the orbit form over four points, C132-WIT-ORBIT), `no_outside_orbit` (over four compact sets, C132-M-ORBIT), `not_ae_outside_points` (the ring form over four points, C132-WIT-EXCL) and `not_ae_outside` (over four compact sets, C132-M-EXCL), `Proofs/HadwigerNelson/Plane/SixRing/Orbit.lean`, each on `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`, resting on no declared outside theorem and taking no assumption in hypothesis form — over the release's palette lemmas, ported with their proofs and kernel-checked here (`Outside/OAI158Palette`, `OAI158CircleMaps` … `OAI158States`). [hand], one mind, with a line-by-line checking reading of its steps M4–M9 by a second mind — the kernel lane's planner, whose own blind route reached the state, the chart, a forced label at each hub, the unit-pair relation and the kill for a clean change, and stopped at the step lemma — for what no Lean theorem states: the statement as written, on an open set containing the closed unit disc (it adds M0, one mind's reading of the release's two proofs as local), and the clause at an extracted centre (C132-M-EMPTY).** **What it stands on:** for a colouring of the whole plane, two statements of `[OAIMath2026]`, kernel-checked there at every number of labels and, ported, here (`Outside.OAI158.palette_transfer_pair`, `palette_sixty_disjoint`) (the transfer of palettes for a pair; the 60° disjointness), and its nine steps M1–M9, each now a kernel theorem; on an open set, as the statement is written, also one mind's reading of those two proofs as local (M0). **Where it sits:** its hypothesis asks an outside label in almost every palette near all six orbit directions — in the map class, no (Q) arc at the orbit; every held positive escapes there (at each of the eight change points of the held fragments' four-colour vertices at least two of the four germs at ±60° lie on (Q) arcs), so nothing held is excluded. It is rows 148–149's exclusion in measure, and covers those rows' fragments given two facts about maps — a map's edge carries the two-colour exclusion (derived for connected open tiles, C132-WITNESS, row 157; the composition unwritten) and a map's palette is its ring read two-sidedly (underived; FS-134). **Not claimed:** "anything when the palettes near one of the six directions ψ + kπ/3 hold no outside label on a set of positive measure (rings with (Q) arcs at the orbit: FS-124)"; "that a six-colouring with hypothesis (a) exists"; "anything at seven labels or more (M2 and M9 spend six)"; "anything about a cycle of three, five or six labels"; "any value of χ(ℝ²)". | 156 | **Cyclic centres exist at every number of colours with no hypothesis; one carries the release's data wherever the centres are finite in a disc; and the step that reads isolation is located:** "for every integer k ≥ 1 and every weak measurable k-colouring of the plane, every closed disc of radius greater than 1 contains a cyclic centre" (C132-CORES-FREE). On a closed disc of radius above 1 with finitely many cyclic centres, some centre in its interior, isolated among them, carries a simple cycle of 3 to `k` labels with, for each consecutive pair, a compact connected set through it, a limiting direction, and the exclusion of both labels' closed essential supports from the set's straddle set — `[OAIMath2026]` Proposition 7.1 read at every `k` and on a disc, with two clauses its manuscript does not state: (4) each set lies in both its labels' supports; (5) each edge of the cycle is an edge of the centre's own transition graph (C132-EXT-DISC; clause (5) is one mind's, against the letter of the manuscript's sentence "The extracted cycle need not consist of edges of Γ_x", and no exclusion reads it). What its argument reads of the hypothesis is zero length of the centres and isolation of the one essential centre; at the island colouring's accumulation centre the conclusion with clause (4) is false. The exclusion clause needs no centre: "let k ≥ 1 be an integer, c a weak measurable k-colouring of the plane, i a label, p_i^ε(z) the proportion of the disc of radius ε about z lying in the class of i, α > 0, (ε_n) a sequence of positive numbers tending to 0, C_n connected sets with p_i^{ε_n} > α at every point of C_n, and K the set of the limits of convergent sequences (z_n) with z_n in C_n; then no point of the closed essential support H_i has one point of K nearer than 1 and another farther than 1" (C132-WIT-SUPER). **GAP:** the data with no hypothesis on the centres (C132-WIT-EX) — stalls at "the selection of a point through which connected pieces of two smoothed classes pass and reach a common second point". The data are free at every point with a margin for three labels, points that are no centres (C132-WIT-MARGIN): a witnessed cycle is a property of the unit circle about a point, a cyclic centre a property of its small balls. **GAP:** a square form of the first statement, side above 1, posed by the kernel planner with a route not written out (C132-CORES-SQUARE). [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending. **Not claimed:** "the conclusion when 𝓛 ∩ D is infinite"; "that the hypothesis holds for any six-colouring"; "that ℓ ≥ 4 at any centre"; "sharpness of the radius 1". **Prior art:** partly — `[OAIMath2026]` Proposition 7.1 with Lemmas 7.2–7.8 at five labels on a square of side ten; the statement at every `k`, the disc, the hypothesis read and clauses (4), (5) not found in print; zbMATH owed. By Kooshi's word (2026-10-07) whether every such colouring has a point with usable data is FS-127's and FS-128's question. | `plans/FS132-derivation.md` §§ 2, 3, 8; `plans/FS132-E1-derivation.md`, `plans/FS132-E5-derivation.md` | FS-132 (2026-10-07) |
| 157 | **One witness blocks one side of a unit circle, with no properness; and two points less than 2 apart carrying witnessed triangles on disjoint triples of six labels force an edge set onto the unit circle about each common unit point — the regular chain's last line in the exact class, its one exception named:** "let U be an open set of the plane, c a measurable map from U to a finite set of labels, H_i the closed essential support of the class of i, E a set of labels, x and q two distinct points with H_i ∩ Δ({x, q}) = ∅ for every i in E, and w a point of U with |w − x| = 1 and |w − q| ≠ 1; then there is η > 0 such that almost every point z of B(w, η) with |z − x| > 1 has its label outside E if |w − q| < 1, and almost every point z of B(w, η) with |z − x| < 1 has its label outside E if |w − q| > 1" (C132-WIT-SIDE; the leaf under the strips, C132-WIT-STRIP and C132-TRI-STRIP: at a point with a witnessed triangle, at every direction but at most six, one side of its unit circle carries only the other labels). The same rule from a piece of the colouring in place of an exclusion set: "let U be an open set of the plane, c a measurable map from U to a finite set of labels with c(z) ≠ c(z + u) for almost every pair of a point z of U and a unit vector u with z + u in U, i a label, p and p′ two points of U with |p − p′| = 1, v a unit vector with v·(p′ − p) ≠ 0, r > 0, and W an open subset of U that contains the points p′ + sv for 0 < s < r and almost every point of which has the label i; then there is η > 0 such that, if v·(p′ − p) > 0, almost every point z of B(p, η) with |z − p′| < 1 has a label other than i, and, if v·(p′ − p) < 0, almost every point z of B(p, η) with |z − p′| > 1 has a label other than i" (C132-SHADOW). A connected open region of one label keeps that label's whole essential support off the straddle set of every compact subset of its closure, so a map's edges are witnessed edges (C132-WITNESS). The covering count at a common unit point reads no properness (C132-CROSS), and: "let U be an open set of the plane, c a measurable map from U to a set of six labels, H_i the closed essential support of the class of i, x and y two points with 0 < |x − y| < 2, t_0, t_1, t_2 and t′_0, t′_1, t′_2 the six labels, K_0, K_1, K_2 compact sets containing x with H_{t_j} ∩ Δ(K_j) = ∅ and H_{t_{j+1}} ∩ Δ(K_j) = ∅ for each j modulo 3, K′_0, K′_1, K′_2 compact sets containing y with H_{t′_j} ∩ Δ(K′_j) = ∅ and H_{t′_{j+1}} ∩ Δ(K′_j) = ∅ for each j modulo 3, and w a point of U with |w − x| = |w − y| = 1; then one of the six sets K_0, K_1, K_2, K′_0, K′_1, K′_2 is contained in the unit circle about w" (C132-COMP-SHARP). Fragment T2 realizes the exception, with three of its six edge sets arcs of the unit circle about the crossing point; so row 133's "tangent pairs or bowties" are, in the exact class, an edge set on the unit circle about the common unit point, and a point with no third edge. **Since FS-124's second round (2026-10-07; FS124-E2, [hand], one mind):** as stated, the sharp form's conclusion is met by a one-point set — the member itself lies on the unit circle about the common unit point — so the form with content is on pieces: of the two members, one has a piece inside the closed unit disc about the point and another disjoint from the open one, their common boundary on that circle (C124-FORCED-PIECES, `plans/FS124-E2-derivation.md`); for members that are three-sector points an arc of an edge lies on it (C124-FORCED); at a bowtie the common boundary can be the member alone. [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending (C132-WIT-SIDE is also the first step of C132-CROSS, written the same night in another record). **Not claimed:** "anything when |w − q| = 1" (C132-WIT-SIDE); "anything when v is tangent at p′ to the unit circle about p" (C132-SHADOW); "which of the six sets it is, or that it is one of x's"; "anything with seven labels or more"; "that a configuration with a set on the unit circle about w is impossible — fragment T2 is one" (C132-COMP-SHARP). **Prior art:** partly — the mechanism of `[OAIMath2026]` Lemma 8.1 for continua; for maps the composition behind row 133's W-TAN; these forms owed. | `plans/FS132-derivation.md` §§ 2, 4; `plans/FS132-E4-derivation.md`, `plans/FS132-E5-derivation.md`, `plans/FS132-E6-derivation.md` | FS-132 (2026-10-07) |
| 158 | **Three labels outside a triangle wind, and six is the one count at which they do; the forcing of a centre that is not a triangle stalls at one circle:** "Let a, b, c be three labels, Θ the union of the edges ab, bc, ca of the simplex of probability vectors, and φ a continuous map from the unit circle S to Θ such that for every e in S no label has a positive coordinate both at φ(e) and at φ(Re); then the degree of φ, for the orientation a → b → c of Θ, is congruent to 3 modulo 6" (C132-INDEX — no colouring in it; so at least nine changes; with two labels the ring is rigid, which is the release's kill at five, and four labels, at seven, have no winding number). The same for a step function with finitely many changes, no two 60° apart (C132-RING-WIND); with no tameness, an orbit index in {2, 3, 4} at almost every direction (C132-ORBIT); the classes of the cyclic centres inside a circle add up to the circle's (C132-REL); and in the complete graph on the labels with every triangle filled every loop bounds, at every `k`, so no obstruction lives there (C132-FILL, C132-NOESS). **GAP:** "There is a radius R₀ > 1 such that, for every weak measurable six-colouring of the plane and every closed disc D of radius R₀ in which its cyclic centres are finitely many, the conclusion of C132-EXT-DISC holds at some point of D with ℓ ≥ 4" (C132-LONG; with it C132-M-CV, an extracted centre with a cycle of at least four labels) — stalls at the winding of the three outside labels on a triangle centre's unit circle, that is at the finiteness of that ring's changes (C132-RING-TAME, first clause: "for three labels on a strip no rigidity is held, by the release or here"; false wherever a colouring has local slack along the strip), and behind it "two further steps of the regular chain have no weak form (two centres of one type at distance between 1 and 2, and the bicycle)". [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending (a second derivation of C132-INDEX by the kernel planner, blind, a paragraph by hand, is not counted). **Not claimed** (C132-INDEX): "anything for a ring that is only measurable"; "for four labels (the target is then a graph of rank three, with no degree)"; "that the ring of any colouring satisfies the hypothesis". **Prior art:** partly — the index of a three-coloured circle and its nine bichromatic points are `[Voronov2023]`'s and `[SokolovVoronov2025]`'s by this project's records; the congruence modulo 6 in this form not searched. | `plans/FS132-derivation.md` §§ 3, 4, 8; `plans/FS132-E2-derivation.md` | FS-132 (2026-10-07) |
| 159 | **What a triangle point's ring is forced to do where it changes — the alternating ring excluded in step form, the turning ring placing a four-label point whose own unit circle runs along two unit arcs — each where no edge set of the centre lies on a unit circle about a ring point:** on a pure side near a ring point the label lies among the three outside labels and outside the palettes of its two ring neighbours (C132-PLACE). The alternating ring `a, b, a, b, a, b` is excluded in step form wherever two adjacent ring points each have a pure side (C132-ALT; for a ring only measurable, derived for two adjacent inner-pure ring points only; its residue is an orbit at which three alternate ring points each have one of the three sets on their unit circle). At a step of a turning ring `a, b, n, a, b, n` whose two neighbours are outer-pure the two ring labels are never a witnessed edge at the ring point (C132-TURN-EDGE). On a turning seed: "let U be an open set of the plane containing the closed unit disc about a point x, c a measurable map from U to six labels with c(z) ≠ c(z + u) for almost every pair of a point z of U and a unit vector u with z + u in U, T a set of three labels and K_0, K_1, K_2 compact sets containing x such that for each of the three pairs of labels of T one of the sets K_j has Δ(K_j) disjoint from the closed essential supports of both labels of the pair, a, b, n the three labels outside T, ψ an angle such that for almost every θ the palette P_x(e(θ)) contains a if θ lies in (ψ − π/3, ψ) or in (ψ + 2π/3, ψ + π), b if θ lies in (ψ, ψ + π/3) or in (ψ + π, ψ + 4π/3), and n if θ lies in (ψ + π/3, ψ + 2π/3) or in (ψ + 4π/3, ψ + 5π/3), and y_k = x + e(ψ + kπ/3); if at least two of the sets K_j have a point strictly inside the unit circle about y_1, then there is η > 0 such that almost every point z of B(y_0, η) with |z − y_1| < 1 has its label outside {a, n}; if at least two of the sets K_j have a point strictly inside the unit circle about y_{−1}, then there is η > 0 such that almost every point z of B(y_0, η) with |z − y_{−1}| < 1 has its label outside {b, n}; and if both hold, there is η > 0 such that almost every point z of B(y_0, η) with |z − y_1| < 1 and |z − y_{−1}| < 1 has its label in T" (C132-LENS — hypotheses at the two neighbours, none at the ring point itself); the middle ring point then has no pure inner side (C132-LENS-SIDE); three consecutive outer-pure ring points make the middle one a point where four labels are present (C132-LENS-FOUR), and three such exist when each of the three sets has a point strictly inside three of the six unit circles about the ring points and strictly outside the other three (C132-LENS-GERM); and the placed point's own unit circle runs, at its two neighbours, along an interface between the two ring labels, oppositely oriented: "let U, x, c, T, K_0, K_1, K_2, a, b, n, ψ and the points y_k be as in C132-LENS, and suppose that at least two of the sets K_j have a point strictly inside the unit circle about y_1 and at least two of them have a point strictly inside the unit circle about y_{−1}; then there is κ > 0 such that almost every point w of B(y_1, κ) with |w − x| > 1 and |w − y_2| > 1 has the label b when |w − y_0| < 1 and the label a when |w − y_0| > 1, and almost every point w of B(y_{−1}, κ) with |w − x| > 1 and |w − y_{−2}| > 1 has the label a when |w − y_0| < 1 and the label b when |w − y_0| > 1; and if U contains the unit circle about y_0, then for almost every θ in (ψ + 2π/3 − κ, ψ + 2π/3) and almost every θ in (ψ − 2π/3, ψ − 2π/3 + κ) the palette P_{y_0}(e(θ)) is contained in {a, b}" (C132-PLACED-RING — a measurable instance of FS-124's first question, every hypothesis derived from the seed's data). **GAPs:** C132-TURN (stalls at a witness at the ring point; not re-posed in that form); C132-LENS-II ("pure sides are opposite at antipodes" holds one way only; sixteen patterns of sides open, seeds whose sets witness from far away); C132-PLACED (whether any pair containing a label of the centre's triple is a witnessed edge at the placed point). [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending. **No model is known at six for any of them** — no weak six-colouring with a turning seed is held — and the one model the day's search kept (row 160) is the other case, to which no statement here applies. **Not claimed:** the existence of a seed; anything at a ring point with no pure side; "anything for an outside state that is not a step at ψ and ψ ± 2π/3" (C132-LENS); and nothing here "excludes, builds or bounds a boundary that is a unit arc about a ring point". **Prior art:** partly — at five labels `[OAIMath2026]`'s lemma "Polynomial three-label region" is the neighbouring statement; in the map class, the ring calculus of row 148; owed in print. | `plans/FS132-derivation.md` §§ 4, 5, 8; `plans/FS132-E4-derivation.md`, `plans/FS132-E6-derivation.md` | FS-132 (2026-10-07) |
| 160 | **In one finite family — a centre and its six ring points, each a *flower* of wedges — the three-label turning ring does not exist, the alternating ring does not exist, and with four labels at alternate ring points the turning ring exists in a shape this project already holds:** the family F is seven flowers; each ring flower is the six 60° wedges cut by the three unit circles through the ring point, one label of six per wedge; the centre's flower is twelve 30° sub-sectors bounded by straight rays or by arcs of unit circles about ring points. "in the seven-flower wedge family F (Stage 1), no model has the turning ring `a, b, n, a, b, n` and at most three labels at every ring point" (SEARCH-1007-TURN3; **[cert]**: four LRAT refutations accepted by `cake_lpr`, re-run by the coordinator). "with `x` simple and straight F has no turning ring at any number of labels" (SEARCH-1007-TURN-SS; [cert], four refutations). "in F the alternating ring `a, b, a, b, a, b` has no model at any number of labels per ring point" (SEARCH-1007-ALT; [cert], sixteen refutations — the positive control of row 159's C132-ALT, not a second derivation of it). "in F, with four labels allowed at ring points, the turning ring has models" (SEARCH-1007-TURN4; [cert] by a cold check of the kept models at two radii): by the search's census — a count, not a certificate — with a simple three-sector centre all 192 models are of one shape, each interface at the centre an arc of the unit circle about a ring point, those three alternate ring points exactly the ones with four labels: fragment T2's shape; local (flowers of radius about 0.25), with nothing between the flowers built. The first null also by a five-step hand argument, for any finite sector structure at the centre (SEARCH-1007-TURN3-HAND; [hand], one mind, unchecked by a second); for germs that are only measurable its step 3's second rule fails, so the null is its family's. **What the nulls are not:** a wedge split along another curve, twelve sectors, unequal sectors, curved sector boundaries, a sector with two outside labels, measurable germs; and nobody but the Searcher has checked that the formulas say what F is. **Prior art:** owed — the family is this project's. | `plans/FS132-derivation.md` § 6; `plans/SEARCH-2026-10-07.md`; `data/search/2026-10-07/` (the proofs' archive, the kept models, `cold_check.py`) | FS-132 (2026-10-07), the Searcher |
| 161 | **The disc criterion, in measure — weak six-colourings of the open discs of every radius give `χ(ℝ²) ≤ 6` with no import, and a disc with none gives seven over one declared outside theorem:** "Let k ≥ 1, U an open set of the plane, c a weak k-colouring of U, and V a finite subset of U. Then the rigid motions g with g(V) ⊂ U form a non-empty open set, and for almost every one of them the points g(v), g(v′) have different labels for every two points v, v′ of V at distance 1; so the unit-distance graph on V has a proper k-colouring." (C124-RADIUS-GRAPH; for a countable set with compact closure in the open set, C124-RADIUS-COUNTABLE). "For every integer k ≥ 1: if for every R > 0 the open disc of radius R about the origin has a weak measurable k-colouring, then every finite unit-distance graph in the plane has a proper k-colouring, and χ(ℝ²) ≤ k." (C124-RADIUS-UP). "For every integer k ≥ 1: if χ(ℝ²) ≤ k, every open set of the plane has a weak measurable k-colouring." (C124-RADIUS-DOWN, over the declared outside theorem `Outside.OAI158.proper_to_borel_weak`). So at each `k` the two hold together or fail together, and at six, with row 33, the plane needs seven colours precisely if some open disc has no weak measurable six-colouring. At five: "There is R > 0 such that no open disc of radius R has a weak measurable five-colouring." (C124-RADIUS-FIVE, over `Outside.OAI158.no_proper_five_coloring`). For a measurable weak colouring every direction is good: "Let U be a measurable subset of the plane and c a measurable map from U to a finite set of labels with c(z) ≠ c(z + u) for almost every pair of a point z of U and a unit vector u with z + u in U. Then for every unit vector u, c(z) ≠ c(z + u) for almost every point z of U with z + u in U." (C124-EVERY-DIRECTION). **Class:** every weak `k`-colouring of an open set, every `k ≥ 1`; no entry reads a number of labels. **Grade:** [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending. **Kernel-checked on a branch, not through the gate, not held** (`fs124-wk1`, 57 theorems): `colorable_iff_forall_ball` and `seven_le_chromaticNumber_plane_iff_exists_ball`, each on the standard three axioms and that one declared outside theorem; (up) and its contrapositive on the standard three alone. The kernel planner's own generalizations there are its module's, not entries: the colouring's measurability idle in the finite-set form, the countable form and (up); (down) for every subset. **Not claimed:** "an explicit radius at any k"; "that the hypothesis holds at k = 6"; "anything without that import" (C124-RADIUS-DOWN); "any value of R" (C124-RADIUS-FIVE). **Prior art:** partly — for a colouring of the whole plane the motion argument is `[GwynStavrianos2022]` Lemma 2 and this library's N-EASY; for arc maps of discs the conclusion is row 145's; the bounded case for proper colourings is Kruskal 2008 (Zbl 1139.05023, held at abstract); (down) is `[OAIMath2026]` family 158's transfer, restricted; the disc form for weak colourings not found in print. | `plans/FS124-derivation.md` § 1; `plans/FS124-E1-derivation.md` Stage 3; branch `fs124-wk1` | FS-124 (2026-10-07) |
| 162 | **What an open piece of one label forces one unit away, in every weak colouring — the one lemma; its form for one connected piece and one side; and what one tight pair forces at its centre, beside its far end and at the opposite point, which is nothing beyond that first step:** "Let U be an open set of the plane, c a measurable map from U to a finite set of labels with c(z) ≠ c(z + u) for almost every pair of a point z of U and a unit vector u with z + u in U, i a label, and W an open subset of U almost every point of which has the label i. Then the set of points of U at distance exactly 1 from some point of W is open, and almost every point of it has a label other than i." (C124-SHADOW-OPEN). "Let c be a weak k-colouring of an open set U, P a connected open subset of U almost every point of which has the label i, x a point of the closure of P, and w a point of U with |w − x| = 1. If P has a point strictly inside S(w), there is η > 0 such that almost every point z of B(w, η) with |z − x| > 1 has a label other than i; if P has a point strictly outside S(w), there is η > 0 such that almost every point z of B(w, η) with |z − x| < 1 has a label other than i." (C124-TYPE — by its deriver, row 157's C132-WITNESS followed by C132-WIT-SIDE in one statement). At a centre, collar by collar: "Let c be a weak k-colouring of an open set U, q and z points of U with |q − z| = 1, ρ > 0 with B(z, ρ) ⊂ U, and a a label. If almost every point of B(z, ρ) with distance less than 1 from q has the label a, there is η > 0 such that almost every point p of B(q, η) with |p − z| > 1 has a label other than a; if almost every point of B(z, ρ) with distance more than 1 from q has the label a, there is η > 0 such that almost every point p of B(q, η) with |p − z| < 1 has a label other than a." (C124-CENTRE-HALF; for a tight pair, both halves: C124-CENTRE). "Let c be a weak k-colouring of U, (q, z) tight for (a, b), and p a point of U with |p − z| = 1, p ≠ q and p ≠ 2z − q. Then there is η > 0 such that almost every point of B(p, η) has a label other than a and other than b." (C124-STRADDLE; at a rhombus completion, C124-COMPLETION). "Let c be a weak k-colouring of U, (q, z) tight for (a, b), and suppose p = 2z − q lies in U. Then there is η > 0 such that almost every point of B(p, η) has a label other than b, and almost every point w of B(p, η) with |w − q| < 2 has a label other than a." (C124-ANTIPODE). The conclusion at a completion "holds beside every interface between two open one-label pieces": read by formula ([tools]), on R7 no completion of an interior far end centres a tight arc (0 of 6) and on FS-112's strip no end of a unit-arc piece centres one (0 of 92). **Class:** every weak `k`-colouring of an open set; **no entry spends a number of labels.** A calibration at seven: "There is a weak measurable seven-colouring of the plane, by formula, with a tight pair (q, z) for (a, b) at which neither a nor b is present at q." (C124-KIND). A count at a centre, at every `k ≥ 4` and over a class with no held member: "Let k ≥ 4, U an open set containing the closed unit disc about q, c a weak k-colouring of U, ψ an angle, z_j = q + e(ψ + jπ/3) for j = 0, …, 5, and a, b, a′, b′ four distinct labels such that for j = 0, 2, 4 the pair (q, z_j) is tight for (a, b) or for (b, a), and for j = 1, 3, 5 the pair (q, z_j) is tight for (a′, b′) or for (b′, a′). Then a label other than a, b, a′, b′ is present at q." (C124-SIX). **Grade:** [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending; C124-SHADOW-OPEN, C124-TYPE, C124-CENTRE-HALF, C124-CENTRE and C124-STRADDLE are in the kernel lane's second module, being planned. **Not claimed:** "anything for a set W that is not open"; "that any weak colouring has an open one-label set"; "anything for a piece that is not connected or not open"; "that any weak colouring has such a piece"; "that the configuration exists at any k" (C124-SIX). **Prior art:** partly — the lemma is the second half of the argument of C132-SHADOW (row 157) and G84's shadow identity for an open piece; in the map class the centre's statement is row 138's cone form; for a weak colouring not found in print; the entries name their debts. | `plans/FS124-derivation.md` §§ 2, 5, 6; `plans/FS124-E1-derivation.md` Stages 4–5; `plans/FS124-E2-derivation.md` Stages 4–5 | FS-124 (2026-10-07) |
| 163 | **What a close complementary pair forces at a common unit point, in measure — on pieces; with an arc where the members are sector points; by both members where they are full, at a point where exactly four labels are present; and the one exception, a member two of whose pieces meet at it only:** "Let c be a weak six-colouring of U, (x, y) a close complementary pair of three-piece points, each with a decomposition, and w a common unit point of the pair lying in U. Then x or y separates at w: one of its pieces lies in the closed unit disc about w and another is disjoint from the open unit disc about w; the edge of those two pieces lies on S(w)." (C124-FORCED-PIECES — the statement with content of row 157's C132-COMP-SHARP, whose conclusion a one-point set meets). "Let c be a weak six-colouring of U, (x, y) a close complementary pair of sector points and w a common unit point of the pair lying in U. Then w is saved by x or by y; and for every interior point z of the arc so obtained the pair (w, z) is tight for the two labels of that edge, in one order along the arc." (C124-FORCED). The same for every three-piece point by the letter is **refuted** (C124-FORCED-AS-POSED; its verdict: "refuted: fragment BB with each bowtie's repeated label joined by a band — both members are three-piece points by the letter, their labels are 1, 2, 3 and 4, 5, 6, they are 1 apart, and no edge of either contains an arc of a circle"). "In the setting of C124-FORCED-PIECES suppose y does not separate at w. Then x has exactly one inner piece, one outer piece and one crossing piece at w, with labels a and b for the inner and the outer one; there are a side of S(y) and η > 0 such that almost every point of B(w, η) on that side has the label a if it lies inside S(x) and the label b if it lies outside S(x); and there are a point w′ ≠ w of S(x) and η′ > 0 such that inside B(x, η′) the inner piece lies in B̄(w, 1) ∩ B̄(w′, 1), the outer piece is disjoint from B(w, 1) ∪ B(w′, 1), and their closures meet at x only." (C124-LONE), and that configuration exists: "There is a weak six-colouring of an open set U, by formula, with a close complementary pair (x, y) of three-piece points and a common unit point w of the pair in U at which y does not separate." (C124-LONE-EXISTS: fragment S1 with a band). "In the setting of C124-FORCED-PIECES, if x and y are full, both separate at w. If they are sector points, each has an edge that contains an arc of S(w) with an end at the member: no common unit point of a close complementary pair of sector points is saved by one member alone." (C124-BOTH). "In the setting of C124-FORCED-PIECES let x and y be full, each with exactly one inner piece, one outer piece and one crossing piece at w. Then exactly four labels are present at w: those of the two inner and the two outer pieces." (C124-FOUR). "In the setting of C124-FORCED for sector points let both common unit points w, w′ lie in U, each with pattern (1, 1). Then the edge of x on the unit circle about w and its edge on the unit circle about w′ are different edges, and the edge of y on the unit circle about w and its edge on the unit circle about w′ are different edges; the labels present at w and those present at w′ are two sets of four with exactly two labels in common, one of each member; their absent pairs are disjoint; and the six labels have a cyclic order in which the three pairs read at w (the two edges, the absent pair) and the three read at w′ alternate." (C124-SECOND). From exclusion sets, the first round's forms: C124-HALF and C124-HALF-TWO, over the inherited C132-WIT-SIDE (the second the upper bound of C124-FOUR). **Class:** three-piece points by the letter (C124-FORCED-PIECES, C124-LONE); full members (C124-BOTH's first sentence, C124-FOUR); sector points (C124-FORCED, C124-BOTH's second sentence, C124-SECOND). **Every entry spends six**; at seven the count fails: "In the hexagonal seven-colouring at circumradius 2/5 there are two vertices less than 2 apart whose label triples are disjoint, with a common unit point interior to a tile of the seventh label; neither vertex separates there and no boundary of the colouring lies on a circle." (C124-FORCED-SEVEN). **Grade:** [hand] or derived from [hand] entries, one mind each, kernel pending; **a second mind is owed on C124-LONE's Step II**, on which C124-BOTH, C124-FOUR and C124-SECOND rest (its deriver's line; the kernel lane's second module is that mind, blind first); one topological input of C124-FORCED is recalled, not read. **Read on held objects** ([tools], one mind; C124-READ-SAVES): at every common unit point inside FS-112's strip and disk and FS-114's capped strip (71, 20, 71) one edge of each member lies on the point's unit circle and exactly four labels are present; fragments T2 and MX and the Searcher's kept model show two edges of one member, fragment BB none, fragment S1 a save by its bowtie alone; FS116-Y2-SIXFRAG has eighteen tight arcs of kind both and no close complementary pair. **Not claimed:** "that a weak six-colouring has a three-piece point, or a close complementary pair"; "anything with seven labels"; "that the saves close up along a chain of pairs; any exclusion" (C124-SECOND); "a value of the chromatic number of the plane" (C124-LONE-EXISTS). **Prior art:** partly — for maps C124-BOTH is FS112-N3-COR3 (row 135; two minds, one blind): "this is its form for a weak measurable colouring and full three-piece points, by another route"; the bowtie as the exception is FS107-W-TAN's (row 133); not found in print. | `plans/FS124-derivation.md` §§ 3, 5–7; `plans/FS124-E2-derivation.md` Stages 3–6; `plans/FS124-E1-derivation.md` Stage 5 | FS-124 (2026-10-07) |
| 164 | **The strip of FS-112 is closed at a point of its top edge against every weak six-colouring that keeps six named pieces — an exclusion of every weak six-colouring of a named open set by the open-piece lemma, local to one held family:** "There is no weak six-colouring of an open set that contains the point w = (1/4, √15/4 + √3/2), the three arches U_{−1}, U_0, U_1 of FS-112's strip and the parts of its tiles T_0, T_1, T_2 below the line y = √15/4, and that gives almost every point of each of these six sets the strip's label." (C124-EDGE-CLOSED): six uses of C124-TYPE (row 162) and exact comparisons in ℚ(√3, √15) bar all six labels on an open set near the point; nothing is assumed above the line `y = √15/4`. As posed, for a save by both members: C124-EDGE. **Class:** one held family — the strip at its own colouring; it spends six ("a seventh label takes the point"). **Grade:** [hand] with an exact check, one mind, kernel pending; it rests on C124-TYPE and on no entry of row 163. **Not claimed:** "anything about colourings that change the strip below y = √15/4"; "that no six-coloured object extends above the strip's top edge". **Prior art:** partly — for extensions of the strip as a map that keep all its tiles, FS112-EDGE (row 136); the held map statement about the strip's six tiles is FS114-Y-STRIPK6 (row 138; two minds, blind) — no valid map contains them, under any colouring — and neither statement is the other; for every weak six-colouring, not found in print. | `plans/FS124-derivation.md` § 4; `plans/FS124-E2-derivation.md` Stage 6 | FS-124 (2026-10-07) |

## B. Theorems proved outside the kernel, by milestone

*One line per theorem a milestone stated and proved by hand, by derivation from held results, or at tools grade — the
intermediate theorems that the rows above compress into prose. Each milestone's `plans/FSnn-derivation.md` holds the full entry
(statement, grade, proof's address, what rests on it, kernel status); this table is the index. Minted 2026-09-22 (Kooshi); backfilled for M17–FS-66 by
the theorem audit (FS-71, row 77) the same day — 254 entries in 53 files, `tools/fs71_ledger_b_check.py --strict` keeps the two in step.*

*From Workflow 3.1 (Kooshi, 2026-10-03) no line per theorem is added here: a milestone's `plans/FSnn-derivation.md` is its theorem record, and this
index ends with FS-116 and FS-121.*
